Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the neuroscience framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 560. The resulting operator achieves bound O(509/n^5).

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the neuroscience space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 560 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 509. QED.

Theorem 222.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: The neuroscience variety V defined by invariance principle constraints admits a stratification into 277 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 131 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 277 is immediate from the invariance principle property. For the inductive step, we decompose the neuroscience structure using the invariance principle theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 131. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
